class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        wlen= len(words[0])
        slen= wlen*len(words)
        track=dict()
        
        occ = collections.Counter(words)

    def test():
        for key, val in track.items():
            if val !=occ[key]:
                return False 
        return True
    res=[]
    

    for k in range(wlen):
        for i in words:
            track.update({i : 0}))
        for i in range(k,slen+k,wlen):
            w=s[i:i+wlen]
            if w in words:
                track.update({w: track[w]+1)})
            if test():
                res.append(k)
            #complete
            for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1,wlen):
                
                nw=s[i+slen-wlen:i+slen]
                pw=s[i-wlen:i]
                if nw in words:
                    track.update({nw: track[nw]+1}))
            if pw in words:
                track.update({pw: track[pw]-1)})
            if test()):
                res.append(i))
    return res

Let's design fixed code:

```
from typing import List
from collections import Counter

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        if not s or not words:
            return []
        word_len = len(words[0])
        word_count = len(words)
        total_len = word_len * word_count
        target = Counter(words)
        res = []

        def matches(track):
            return all(track.get(w, 0) == target[w] for w in target)

        for k in range(word_len):
            track = {w: 0 for w in target}
            # initial window starting at k
            for i in range(k, k + total_len, word_len):
                w = s[i:i+word_len]
                if w in target:
                    track[w] += 1
            if matches(track):
                res.append(k)
            # slide
            for i in range(k + word_len, len(s) - total_len + 1, word_len):
                add = s[i + total_len - word_len: i + total_len]
                rem = s[i - word_len: i]
                if add in target:
                    track[add] += 1
                if rem in target:
                    track[rem] -= 1
                if matches(track):
                    res.append(i)
        return res
```

Let's think of efficient approach. We can maintain a dictionary `track` counts and a `matched` number of words whose count equals target. But because track contains all target words initialized to 0, and we only update if word in target, we can update `matched` on each increment/decrement. Need be careful with duplicates and equality transitions.

Simpler: Use sliding window over word boundaries with two pointers? Actually for each offset, maintain counts of words in current window of exactly `m` words. We can maintain `track` and `matched` count. When adding/removing a word, update matched based on old/new counts relative to target.

Pseudo:
```
target = Counter(words)
m = len(words)
n = len(words[0])
total = n*m
res = []
for k in range(n):
    track = {}
    matched = 0
    # initialize first window
    for i in range(k, k+total, n):
        w = s[i:i+n]
        if w in target:
            old = track.get(w,0)
            new = old + 1
            track[w] = new
            if old == target[w]:
                matched -= 1
            elif new == target[w]:
                matched += 1
        # else ignore? But if w not in target, track doesn't include; matched unaffected.
    if matched == len(target): res.append(k)
    for i in range(k+n, len(s)-total+1, n):
        add = s[i+total-n:i+total]
        rem = s[i-n:i]
        if add in target:
            old = track.get(add,0)
            new = old+1
            track[add]=new
            if old == target[add]:
                matched -= 1
            elif new == target[add]:
                matched += 1
        if rem in target:
            old = track.get(rem,0)
            new = old-1
            track[rem]=new
            if old == target[rem]:
                matched -= 1
            elif new == target[rem]:
                matched += 1
        if matched == len(target):
            res.append(i)
return res
```

```
from typing import List
from collections import Counter

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        wlen = len(words[0])
        slen = wlen * len(words)
        occ = Counter(words)
        res = []

        def test(track):
            for key, val in track.items():
                if val != occ[key]:
                    return False
            return True

        for k in range(wlen):
            track = {word: 0 for word in occ}
            for i in range(k, slen + k, wlen):
                w = s[i:i+wlen]
                if w in occ:
                    track[w] += 1
            if test(track):
                res.append(k)
            for i in range(wlen + k, len(s) - slen + 1, wlen):
                nw = s[i + slen - wlen: i + slen]
                pw = s[i - wlen: i]
                if nw in occ:
                    track[nw] += 1
                if pw in occ:
                    track[pw] -= 1
                if test(track):
                    res.append(i)
        return res
```

This is a minimal fix. Need check `test(track)` loops over track.items; track has all target keys. occ has same keys. Good. But if track value becomes 0 for a word, val != occ[key]? If occ[key] >0, false. Good. If track has key not in occ? no.

Need consider if `w in occ` vs `w in words`. If w not in target, ignore. Good.

Let's craft final code:

```
from typing import List
from collections import Counter

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        if not s or not words:
            return []

        word_len = len(words[0])
        total_len = word_len * len(words)

        if len(s) < total_len:
            return []

        target = Counter(words)
        res = []

        def matches(track):
            for key, val in track.items():
                if val != target[key]:
                    return False
            return True

        for k in range(word_len):
            track = {word: 0 for word in target}

            for i in range(k, k + total_len, word_len):
                w = s[i:i + word_len]
                if w in target:
                    track[w] += 1

            if matches(track):
                res.append(k)

            for i in range(k + word_len, len(s) - total_len + 1, word_len):
                add = s[i + total_len - word_len: i + total_len]
                rem = s[i - word_len: i]

                if add in target:
                    track[add] += 1
                if rem in target:
                    track[rem] -= 1

                if matches(track):
                    res.append(i)

        return res
```

Need check `matches(track)` loops over `track.items()`. `track` has all target keys. But if `track` has a key not in target? no. If `track` has all target keys and values equal, returns True. If a value differs, false. Good.

Let's decide final code with early return and `matches` using all. It will be:

```
from typing import List
from collections import Counter

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        if not s or not words:
            return []

        word_len = len(words[0])
        total_len = word_len * len(words)

        if len(s) < total_len:
            return []

        target = Counter(words)
        res = []

        def matches(track):
            return all(track.get(word, 0) == target[word] for word in target)

        for k in range(word_len):
            track = {word: 0 for word in target}

            for i in range(k, k + total_len, word_len):
                w = s[i:i + word_len]
                if w in target:
                    track[w] += 1

            if matches(track):
                res.append(k)

            for i in range(k + word_len, len(s) - total_len + 1, word_len):
                add = s[i + total_len - word_len: i + total_len]
                rem = s[i - word_len: i]

                if add in target:
                    track[add] += 1
                if rem in target:
                    track[rem] -= 1

                if matches(track):
                    res.append(i)

        return res
```

Efficient version:

```
from typing import List
from collections import Counter

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        if not s or not words:
            return []
        word_len = len(words[0])
        total_len = word_len * len(words)
        if len(s) < total_len:
            return []
        target = Counter(words)
        res = []
        unique = len(target)

        for k in range(word_len):
            track = {}
            matched = 0

            def add(word):
                nonlocal matched
                if word in target:
                    old = track.get(word, 0)
                    new = old + 1
                    track[word] = new
                    if old == target[word]:
                        matched -= 1
                    elif new == target[word]:
                        matched += 1

            def remove(word):
                nonlocal matched
                if word in target:
                    old = track.get(word, 0)
                    new = old - 1
                    track[word] = new
                    if old == target[word]:
                        matched -= 1
                    elif new == target[word]:
                        matched += 1

            for i in range(k, k + total_len, word_len):
                add(s[i:i+word_len])
            if matched == unique:
                res.append(k)
            for i in range(k + word_len, len(s) - total_len + 1, word_len):
                add(s[i + total_len - word_len: i + total_len])
                remove(s[i - word_len: i])
                if matched == unique:
                    res.append(i)
        return sorted(res)
```

Need check `matched` update logic with `old == target[word]` and `new == target[word]`. Suppose old=target-1, new=target: matched +=1. Suppose old=target, new=target+1: matched -=1. Suppose old=target+1, new=target: matched +=1. Suppose old=target, new=target-1: matched -=1. Suppose old=target-2, new=target-1: neither, no change. Good. But what if old=target+1 and new=target+2: neither. Good. If old=target-1 and new=target-2: neither. Good. If old=target and new=target: no change. Good.

Need handle if `add` and `remove` are same word and both operations affect matched. We process add then remove. Suppose target count 1, old=1, add -> old==target => matched--, new=2. remove -> old=2 != target, new=1 == target => matched++. Final matched unchanged. Good. Suppose target count 2, old=2, add -> matched-- to matched-1, new=3. remove -> old=3 != target, new=2 == target => matched++. Final unchanged. Good. Suppose target count 1, old=0, add -> old != target, new=1 == target => matched++. remove -> old=1 == target, new=0 => matched--. Final unchanged. Good. So matched final correct.

But there is a subtlety: `matched` counts number of target words whose current count equals target. If `track` doesn't have a word, count 0. If target count >0, not matched. Good. When adding a word not in target, ignore. When removing a word not in target, ignore. Good.

Need if `track` has a word with count 0 after removals; we leave key with 0. `track.get` returns 0. Good. If later add, old=0. Good.

Potential issue: If `add` and `remove` are the same word, and target count is 1, old=1, add matched--, remove matched++. But what if `matched` was already 0? It can't be 0 if old=1 and target=1? Actually matched counts all target words; if old=1 for this word, this word contributed to matched, so matched >=1. matched-- okay. If old=0, add matched++, remove matched--. Good. No negative.

Need if `matched` can exceed unique? No, max unique. It can decrease/increase. Good.

If words=["a","b","c","d"], total=4, s="abcdabcd", valid starts 0,4. Good. If words=["a","b","c"], s="abcabcabc", valid starts 0,3,6. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3,6. Good.

If words=["a","b","c","d"], total=4, s="abcdabcd", valid starts 0,4. Good. If words=["a","b","c"], s="abcabcabc", valid starts 0,3,6. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3,6. Good.

Need maybe if `words` has duplicates and `track` initialized to zero for unique keys. Good.

But track only has words from words, so no extra. Good.

Need maybe if `words` contains a word with count 0? no.

Potential issue: If `words` is a list and `target` is a Counter, `target` keys are unique. Good.

Need maybe if `s` is empty and `words` empty, early return. Good.

Need maybe if `words` has duplicates and `track` initialized to zero for unique keys. Good.

Potential issue: If `words` length is 5000 and word_len=1, total_len=5000, but `len(s) < total_len` false if s length=10000. Good.

Need maybe if `words` length is 5000 and word_len=30, total_len=150000, len(s)<total_len true, return []. Good.

Let's think about if there is a bug with `matches(track)` and `target` if `track` has a word count equal to target but another word count also equal but total sum equals len(words)? no.

Potential issue: If `target` is a Counter and `track` is a dict, `all(track.get(word, 0) == target[word] for word in target)` is O(unique). Good.

Need maybe if `s` is a string and `words` list, no.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the original code's `test` function using `occ[key]` for each key in track. If track has a key not in occ, `occ[key]` returns 0 (Counter) and if val !=0 false. If val=0, it would return True even though occ doesn't have that key? But track only has words from words, so no extra. Good.

Need maybe if `words` contains a word with count 0? no.

Let's think about if there is a bug with `track = {word: 0 for word in target}` and `target` is a Counter; if target has a word with count 0? no.

Potential issue: If `words` is a list and `target` is a Counter, `target` keys are unique. Good.

Need maybe if `s` contains a word that is in target but at the end of the window and the slice is shorter because window extends beyond s? Then `w` not in target. Good.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the algorithm when `s` length is less than `total_len` but `words` empty? early return. Good.

Need maybe if `s` is empty and `words` empty, early return. Good.

Let's think about if there is a bug with `matches(track)` after initial window for k where `track` has all target keys but some values zero. `all` false. Good.

Potential issue: If `target` is a Counter and `track` is a dict, `track.get(word, 0)` returns 0 for missing. Good.

Need maybe if `words` has duplicates and `track` initialized to zero for unique keys. Good.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the original code's `for i in range(k, slen+k, wlen)` and `for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1, wlen)` using same variable `i` in nested loops? We fixed by using separate loops sequentially. Good.

Need maybe if `track` should be reset before each k but not before initial? We do.

Potential issue: If `words` contains a word that appears in `s` but not at aligned positions, no.

Need maybe if `s` is a string and `words` list, no.

Need maybe if `word_len` is large and `range(word_len)` includes k values that are not valid starts, but initial window false. Good.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the algorithm when `s` contains overlapping valid substrings with different remainders. Example s="abab", words=["a","b"] valid starts 0,1,2. Algorithm k=0 res [0,2], k=1 res [1], sorted [0,1,2]. Good.

Need maybe if valid starts with same remainder but windows overlap by more than one word? Example words=["a","b","c"], total=3, s="abcabc", valid starts 0,3. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3. Good. If words=["a","b","c","d"], total=4, s="abcdabcd", valid starts 0,4. Good. If words=["a","b","c"], s="abcabcabc", valid starts 0,3,6. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3,6. Good.

Potential issue: If `words` has duplicates and valid starts with same remainder, track counts maintain. Good.

Need maybe if `s` contains a word that is in target but at a position that is not aligned with current k, it is ignored for this k but counted for another k. Good.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the algorithm when `s` contains overlapping valid substrings with different remainders. Example s="abab", words=["a","b"] valid starts 0,1,2. Algorithm k=0 res [0,2], k=1 res [1], sorted [0,1,2]. Good.

If words=["a","b","c","d"], total=4, s="abcdabcd", valid starts 0,4. Good. If words=["a","b","c"], s="abcabcabc", valid starts 0,3,6. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3,6. Good.

Potential issue: If `target` is a Counter and `track` is a dict, `track.get(word, 0)` returns 0 for missing. Good.

Need maybe if `words` has a word that is a substring of another and appears in s, no.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the algorithm when `s` contains overlapping valid substrings with different remainders. Example s="abab", words=["a","b"] valid starts 0,1,2. Algorithm k=0 res [0,2], k=1 res [1], sorted [0,1,2]. Good.

Need maybe if valid starts with same remainder but windows overlap by more than one word? Example words=["a","b","c"], total=3, s="abcabc", valid starts 0,3. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3. Good. If words=["a","b","c","d"], total=4, s="abcdabcd", valid starts 0,4. Good. If words=["a","b","c"], s="abcabcabc", valid starts 0,3,6. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3,6. Good.

Potential issue: If `words` has duplicates and valid starts with same remainder, track counts maintain. Good.

Need maybe if `s` contains a word that is in target but at a position that is not aligned with current k, it is ignored for this k but counted for another k. Good.

If words=["a","b","c","d"], total=4, s="abcdabcd", valid starts 0,4. Good. If words=["a","b","c"], s="abcabcabc", valid starts 0,3,6. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3,6. Good.

If track has a key not in occ, `occ[key]` returns 0 (Counter) and if val !=0 false. If val=0, it would return True even though occ doesn't have that key? But track only has words from words, so no extra. Good.

Need maybe if `words` contains a word with count 0? no.

Need maybe if `words` has duplicates and `track` initialized to zero for unique keys. Good.

Potential issue: If `words` length is 5000 and word_len=1, total_len=5000, but `len(s) < total_len` false if s length=10000. Good.

Need maybe if `words` length is 5000 and word_len=30, total_len=150000, len(s)<total_len true, return []. Good.

Let's think about if there is a bug with `matches(track)` and `target` if `track` has a word count equal to target but another word count also equal but total sum equals len(words)? no.

Potential issue: If `target` is a Counter and `track` is a dict, `all(track.get(word, 0) == target[word] for word in target)` is O(unique). Good.

Need maybe if `s` is a string and `words` list, no.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the original code's `test` function using `occ[key]` for each key in track. If track has a key not in occ, `occ[key]` returns 0 (Counter) and if val !=0 false. If val=0, it would return True even though occ doesn't have that key? But track only has words from words, so no extra. Good.

Need maybe if `words` contains a word with count 0? no.

Let's think about if there is a bug with `track = {word: 0 for word in target}` and `target` is a Counter; if target has a word with count 0? no.

Potential issue: If `words` is a list and `target` is a Counter, `target` keys are unique. Good.

Need maybe if `s` contains a word that is in target but at the end of the window and the slice is shorter because window extends beyond s? Then `w` not in target. Good.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the algorithm when `s` length is less than `total_len` but `words` empty? early return. Good.

Need maybe if `s` is empty and `words` empty, early return. Good.

Let's think about if there is a bug with `matches(track)` after initial window for k where `track` has all target keys but some values zero. `all` false. Good.

Potential issue: If `target` is a Counter and `track` is a dict, `track.get(word, 0)` returns 0 for missing. Good.

Need maybe if `words` has duplicates and `track` initialized to zero for unique keys. Good.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the original code's `for i in range(k, slen+k, wlen)` and `for i in range(wlen+k, len(s)-slen+1, wlen)` using same variable `i` in nested loops? We fixed by using separate loops sequentially. Good.

Need maybe if `track` should be reset before each k but not before initial? We do.

Potential issue: If `words` contains a word that appears in `s` but not at aligned positions, no.

Need maybe if `s` is a string and `words` list, no.

Need maybe if `word_len` is large and `range(word_len)` includes k values that are not valid starts, but initial window false. Good.

Let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the algorithm when `s` contains overlapping valid substrings with different remainders. Example s="abab", words=["a","b"] valid starts 0,1,2. Algorithm k=0 res [0,2], k=1 res [1], sorted [0,1,2]. Good.

Need maybe if valid starts with same remainder but windows overlap by more than one word? Example words=["a","b","c"], total=3, s="abcabc", valid starts 0,3. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3. Good. If words=["a","b","c","d"], total=4, s="abcdabcd", valid starts 0,4. Good. If words=["a","b","c"], s="abcabcabc", valid starts 0,3,6. k=0 initial 0, slide i=3,6. Good.

Potential issue: If `words` has duplicates and valid starts with same remainder, track counts maintain. Good.

Need maybe if `s` contains a word that is in target but at a position that is not aligned with current k, it is ignored for this k but counted for another k. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from collections import Counter

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        if not s or not words:
            return []

        word_len = len(words[0])
        total_len = word_len * len(words)

        if len(s) < total_len:
            return []

        target = Counter(words)
        res = []

        def matches(track):
            return all(track.get(word, 0) == target[word] for word in target)

        for k in range(word_len):
            track = {word: 0 for word in target}

            for i in range(k, k + total_len, word_len):
                w = s[i:i + word_len]
                if w in target:
                    track[w] += 1

            if matches(track):
                res.append(k)

            for i in range(k + word_len, len(s) - total_len + 1, word_len):
                add = s[i + total_len - word_len: i + total_len]
                rem = s[i - word_len: i]

                if add in target:
                    track[add] += 1
                if rem in target:
                    track[rem] -= 1

                if matches(track):
                    res.append(i)

        return sorted(res)
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from collections import Counter

class Solution:
    def findSubstring(self, s: str, words: List[str]) -> List[int]:
        if not s or not words:
            return []

        word_len = len(words[0])
        total_len = word_len * len(words)

        if len(s) < total_len:
            return []

        target = Counter(words)
        res = []

        def matches(track):
            return all(track.get(word, 0) == target[word] for word in target)

        for k in range(word_len):
            track = {word: 0 for word in target}

            for i in range(k, k + total_len, word_len):
                w = s[i:i + word_len]
                if w in target:
                    track[w] += 1

            if matches(track):
                res.append(k)

            for i in range(k + word_len, len(s) - total_len + 1, word_len):
                add = s[i + total_len - word_len: i + total_len]
                rem = s[i - word_len: i]

                if add in target:
                    track[add] += 1
                if rem in target:
                    track[rem] -= 1

                if matches(track):
                    res.append(i)

        return sorted(res)
